--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of marriage compatibility from page 26. While the previous page focused on biological types (Yoni) and temperaments (Gana), this page details the "Kuta" or specific mathematical counts between the zodiac signs and constellations of the couple, specifically addressing the 6-8, 2-12, and 5-9 relationships.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>
<header>Mu-Ma- 27 / Commentary- 27</header>

Friendship exists when there is a match between two divine natures or between a divine and a human nature. Now he explains the matching <note>original: "kuta"</note> of the zodiac signs. In the 2-12 relationship <term>Dvirdasha</term> <note>where one sign is the 2nd and the other is the 12th from each other</note>, there is a lack of wealth and a disposition toward misfortune. In the 6-8 relationship <term>Shadashtaka</term>, there is calamity and death. In the 5-9 relationship <term>Navapanchama</term>, there is "unevenness" <note>original: "vaishumya"</note> which results in childlessness. In the remaining cases—specifically the 3-11, 4-10, and 1-7 relationships—various benefits such as wealth, children, and cattle arise. 

In what condition do these remaining cases apply? They apply when the constellation quarter <term>nakshatra-charana</term> is not identical. It is taught that an inauspicious result occurs when the matching involves the exact same constellation quarter. Regarding marriage in the same zodiac sign: while a single sign is generally auspicious for marriage, it is also said that a single sign can grant death <note>depending on the specific constellation within it</note>. There are four variations of the "single sign" rule:
1. Same sign but different constellations.
2. Same constellation but different signs.
3. Same sign and constellation but different quarters.
4. Same sign, constellation, and same quarter.

->Friendship exists between divine natures, and between human and divine natures. Poverty occurs in the 2-12 relation; death and calamity in the 6-8 relation; childlessness in the 5-9 relation. Wealth arises in the rest.<-
->When the constellations are different or the signs are the same, or the constellation is the same but the signs are different, one does not consider the flaws of temperament, pulse, or distance. There are 6 variations. There, the same sign, constellation, and quarter is specified...<-

...as "identical constellation quarters" <note>original: "vibhaika-charana"</note>. Now, regarding auspicious matching: if the constellation and sign are the same, or if the signs are the same but constellations differ, one should not worry about the flaws of temperament <term>Gana</term>, pulse <term>Nadi</term>, or distance <term>Dura</term>. This means that despite such flaws, the match remains auspicious. As stated by the sage Brihaspati: "When there is a single sign with different constellations, or a single constellation with different signs, one should not worry about temperament, pulse, distance, or planetary enmity." In the *Kala-nirnaya*, it is said: "For a couple, the same constellation with different quarters brings auspiciousness; however, a couple sharing the same constellation quarter faces certain death within half a year." 6 

Now he explains the acceptable 6-8 and 2-12 relationships. In a 6-8 relationship <term>Shadashtaka</term>, if it involves an "even" sign <term>Samabha</term> such as Cancer, Virgo, etc., and is combined with the 6th sign, it is considered beautiful and acceptable. For example, Taurus is an even sign; if it is paired with the 6th sign from it, which is Libra, it is acceptable. This is because there is no planetary enmity between their lords. However, an even sign paired with the 8th is not acceptable. This should be understood through the reverse logic as well.

->In the 6-8 relation, if it is an even sign and combined with the 6th, it is beautiful. An even sign in a 2-12 relation combined with the 2nd is also acceptable. Leo is an exception; it is auspicious when paired with Virgo.<-
->The "Pulse" constellations are: Mula, Jyeshtha, Hasta, Shatabhisha, the first quarter of Purvabhadrapada, Ashwini, Ardra, Punarvasu, Pushya, Ashlesha, Magha, Purvaphalguni, Uttaraphalguni, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, Anuradha... 7 If the couple shares the same pulse in these nine constellations, death occurs. In the south of the Godavari river, some say the "side pulse" is beneficial. Through friendship, 2-12 and 6-8 relations are neutralized by the power of the master. 8<-

Now he describes the acceptable 2-12 relations <term>Dvirdasha</term>. When an even sign is combined with the 2nd, the remainder is auspicious. If the opposite occurs, it is inauspicious. Here, he mentions a specific exception for the sign Leo: although it might be difficult, Leo paired with Virgo <note>the 2nd sign from it</note> is excellent. 

Now he explains the pulse <term>Nadi</term> matching starting with the word "Mula." In this verse, the three pulses <note>the three "channels" or "pulses" used to determine physiological compatibility</note> are indicated by three sets of constellations. <term>Mula</term> is well known; <term>Indra</term> refers to the constellation Jyeshtha; <term>Arka-bha</term> is Hasta; <term>Pashi</term> is Shatataraka; <term>Aja-ika-charana</term> is the first quarter of Purvabhadrapada...

<summary>This page explains the astrological "Kuta" or matching rules for marriage, detailing why certain house positions like 6-8 and 2-12 are usually avoided but identifying specific exceptions where they become auspicious. It also introduces the Nadi (pulse) system of constellation groups to ensure health and longevity for the couple.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of marriage compatibility from page 27, moving from the mathematical relationship between zodiac signs to the "Pulse" (Nadi) compatibility and the specific ritual remedies (donations) required to offset inauspicious astrological alignments.</meta>

<page-num>28</page-num>
<header>Su. Bha. / Commentary</header>

<margin>Su. Bha. 28</margin>
The constellations <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions used to calculate destiny</note> including Punarvasu, Uttara Phalguni, Ardra, and Ashwini form one "Pulse" <term>Nadi</term>. The constellations Bharani, Mrigashira, Pushya, Anuradha, Purva Phalguni, Dhanishta, Chitra, Purva Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada also form a group where they share a single pulse. There are three such pulses in total. In the context of marriage, when the constellations of the bride and groom fall within the same pulse <term>Eka-nadi</term>, it results in death <note>original: "mrityu"</note>. 

However, in the southern regions such as near the Gautami <note>the Godavari river</note>, for those of the warrior class <term>Kshatriya</term> and others, or when another suitable bride cannot be found, a specific rule applies: the "Side Pulse" <term>Parshva-nadi</term> is considered beneficial and auspicious. The "Middle Pulse" <term>Madhya-nadi</term> is the most critical; if the pulses conflict at the front, the husband is affected; if in the middle, both suffer; if at the back, the wife is affected—of this there is no doubt. Even if the distance between constellations is small, if the pulses conflict, it results in the corruption of the lineage within a year. Regarding the priestly class <term>Brahmins</term>, the *Light of Astrology* <note>original: "Jyotih-prakasha"</note> states that death occurs in the middle pulse, but not necessarily in the side pulses. This arrangement is also stated in the *Rigveda* followers' tradition: for the warrior class and others south of the Gautami river, a conflict in the rear pulse is avoided.

Now he discusses the auspiciousness of the previously mentioned 2-12 relationship <term>Dvir-dvadasha</term> <note>where signs are adjacent</note>. Through the friendship of the ruling planets <term>Graha-maitri</term>, even the 2-12, 5-9, or 6-8 relationships can become auspicious. If there is mutual attraction and the six types of compatibility (including pulse and temperament) are met, the 6-8 relationship <term>Shadashtaka</term> is considered good. If the woman is of the "Human" temperament <term>Manushya-gana</term> and the man is of the "Demon" temperament <term>Rakshasa-gana</term>, then—

---

<margin>Commentary 28</margin>
—the match is auspicious if the seven other compatibility factors <term>Kutas</term> and planetary friendships are present. [7-8] Furthermore, if the woman is of the "Demon" temperament and the man is of the "Human" temperament, the union is good if joined by the six factors including planetary obedience and pulse compatibility. Now, he explains the exception for "Man-Distance" <term>Nridura</term> <note>the distance between the couple's constellations</note>. If the man's constellation is near the woman's, it is usually inauspicious; however, it becomes auspicious if there is planetary friendship and constellation friendship <term>Yoni-maitri</term>. 

Next, he describes the ritual donations <term>Dana</term> to be given during a crisis of "corrupt compatibility":

> In the 2-12 relation, one should give copper and gold. In the 6-8 relation, a pair of cows. In the 5-9 relation, silver and bell-metal. If they share the same pulse <term>Eka-nadi</term>, one should give a cow, gold, and other gifts before marrying. [9]

Specifically, for the 2-12 relationship, donate copper and gold. For the 6-8 relationship, donate a pair of cows. For the 5-9 relationship, donate silver and bell-metal. Regarding the pulse conflict: one should give a cow, gold, many clothes, and food before marrying. The text *The Sun of Kings* <note>original: "Rajamartanda"</note> confirms: give a pair of cows for the 6-8, bell-metal for the 5-9, and for the pulse conflict, give a milch cow, food, gold, and clothes. For the 2-12, satisfy the Brahmins with copper and gold to offset the loss of wealth. <meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of marriage compatibility, transitioning from the structural "Pulse" (Nadi) of the previous page to the "Analysis of Merits" (Guna-vichara), specifically focusing on Social Class (Varna), Attraction (Vashya), and Friendship (Mitra).</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>
<header>Su. Ma. Ti. 29</header>

Now, the author explains the "Analysis of Merits" <term>Guna-vichara</term> <note>the system of assigning points to various astrological factors for compatibility</note> through three verses. First, he addresses the merit of Social Class <term>Varna-guna-vichara</term>. It is understood that one point is granted for "Equality of Class" <note>original: "Varna-aikya"</note>. When the classes are the same, or when the groom is of a superior class <note>original: "Vara-uttama"</note>, one point is assigned in both cases. By implication, if the groom is of a lower class than the bride, there is an absence of merit (zero points). As stated in the *Astrological Compendium* <note>original: "Jyotisha-nibandha"</note>: "One point is given for the same class and for a superior groom; for a lower class groom, the merit is zero." Some also say a "similar half" applies.

Next, the author explains the merit of "Attraction or Control" <term>Vashya-guna-vichara</term>. In the case of a "Subservient-Prey" relationship <term>Vashya-bhakshya</term>, a half-point (0.5) is granted. For instance, among humans, aquatic creatures are considered subservient prey; in such cases, a half-point is taken. This should be understood regarding the "Twice-born" <term>Dvija</term> <note>the upper three social classes: Brahmins, Kshatriyas, and Vaishyas</note>. The twice-born sometimes eat fish of the *Pathina* variety during ancestral rites <term>Shraddha</term>, though not all do so. Therefore, one must consider the specific sub-caste <note>original: "jati"</note> of the groom when applying this. Among the lower-

### Class equality, groom's superiority, and subservience-prey relationships earn half a point; for mutual friends, two; in the enemy-food category, points are one, six, and three.

-classes, the merit is zero, as stated in the same verse: "in the case of one's own enemy-food." Likewise, all are subservient and prey to the Lion <note>Leo</note>. Among these, humans are considered "Enmity-food" <note>original: "vairi-bhakshya"</note>, while the Scorpion <note>Scorpio</note> is a natural enemy; others are simply subservient prey. 

Next, for two "Friends" <term>Mitra</term>—those who are mutually well-disposed—two points are understood. The analysis here is that friendship is evident within one's own class. Among humans, there is friendship with forest animals <note>original: "parshvamrigaih"</note>. Among the lower classes, animals are considered subservient prey and thus receive a half-point; elsewhere, two points. 

Next, in the case of "Enemy-Subservience" <term>Ari-vashake</term>, the merit is one <note>original: "dhara" - literally 'Earth', signifying the number 1</note>. An example of an enemy-subservient relationship is the Scorpion for humans. There is an eternal enmity between the Lion and the Scorpion; it is famous in the world that a lion meets its death when bitten by a scorpion. Therefore, there is an absence of merit here. It was previously stated that "one eats the other from among people," so the wise must speak only after careful consideration.

---

This is the insight that has flashed in my mind regarding the analysis of Attraction <term>Vashya</term>. What is stated here should be refined by the learned. One might ask: "Is it not said that in the subservience-food relationship, the Lion is not a controller but a killer?" How can it be said that humans are "Enemy-food" for the Lion? It is true; that is a specific worldly characteristic, but there is a general rule. Thus the author says "from other people." The Lion is eager to kill even humans and elephants because of its power, but it is indifferent to killing others because they are not its equal. As it is said: "In the case of enmity and attraction, there is an absence of merit; if both are subservient, two points; in attraction-enmity, half a point; and in subservience-food, half a point." 

Next, he explains the merit of the "Birth Stars" <term>Tara-guna-vichara</term>. When there is movement from both sides <note>both horoscopes</note>, there are three points <note>original: "agnayah" - literally 'Fires', signifying the number 3</note>. This means if, for example, the star *Hasta* is involved, and there is movement from both sides—

### For mixed portions, or for great friends, four and three points; for friends, one; for two enemies, two; for great enemies, zero. (10)

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the "Eight-fold Grid" (Ashta-kuta) used to determine marriage compatibility. It transitions from the "Star" (Tara) merit to "Species" (Yoni), "Planetary Friendship" (Graha-maitri), and "Temperament" (Gana).</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>
<header>Su. Ma. 30</header>
<header>Di.</header>
<meta>Catchword: Ya</meta>

...thus, these are the merits of the stars <note>referring to the *Tara-guna* from the previous page</note>. The term "mixed" <note>original: *mishra*</note> means that on one side the star is auspicious, and on the other, it is inauspicious. In such a mixed case, the merit for that portion of the star is one-and-a-half points; otherwise, there is a total absence of merit. As it is said: "When auspiciousness is obtained from one side and inauspiciousness from the other, the merit is one-and-a-half. When both stars are pure, there are three points. When neither star is auspicious, one should declare it to be zero <note>original: *shunya*</note>."

**Now, the analysis of the Merit of Species <term>Yoni-guna-vichara</term> is stated.**

If the species of the couple are identical, the merit is four points <note>original: *veda*</note>. For friendly species, it is three points. For neutral species <note>original: *svabhava*</note>, it is two points. For opposing species, one point. For "Great Enemies" <term>Mahad-vaira</term>, the merit is zero. This is the breakdown of the merits of the Species <term>Yoni</term>. 

The analysis is as follows: there are fourteen species assigned to the twenty-seven birth stars <note>original: *nakshatras*</note>, such as the Horse <term>Ashva</term> and others. Regarding what was said about "Lion, Buffalo, and so on," there is "Great Enmity." Specifically, the Buffalo and Horse, and the Monkey and Sheep, are considered "Great Enemies" <note>original: *mahad-vaira*</note> and naturally lack mutual compatibility. 

Furthermore, even among the same species, one must recognize "Great Friends" and those who are not. Among Elephants, Dogs, Cats, Tigers, and Lions—even if they share a species category—they may not be friends due to natural causes for mutual quarreling. These five pairs are considered to have "natural" <note>neutral</note> merit. The seven pairs designated as "Great Enemies" include the Buffalo and Horse, and the Monkey and Sheep. 

A Tiger is neutral toward Snakes and Mongooses; a Lion is similarly neutral. Furthermore, these two (Tiger and Lion) are enemies with others. There is enmity between the Cat and the Rat, friendship between the Dog and the Sheep, and friendship between the Cow and the Buffalo. There is enmity between the Rat and the Snake. One should determine the merits after considering these states of enemy, friend, and neutral. In cases of neutrality, one takes the "natural" merit level. Although the merits for neutrals are not explicitly listed in every case, neutrality is observed between the Buffalo, Horse, Sheep, and Monkey; therefore, it is understood that the "natural" merit should be applied to them. As it is said: "For Great Enemies, Enemies, Neutrals, Friends, and Great Friends, the points are seen as zero, one, two, three, and four respectively."

---

**Now, the analysis of the Merit of Planetary Lords <term>Svechara-guna-vichara</term> is stated.**

When there is "One Lord" <term>Ekesha</term> (the same ruling planet for both birth signs) or "Mutual Friends," five points <note>original: *shara* (arrows of Kama)</note> are taken. In the case of "Both Friends," five points are also taken. 

> For the same lord or mutual friends, five points; half for neutral-enemies; 
> four for neutral-friends; one for an enemy-friend combination; 
> and two for those that are neutral. 

Where merits are not mentioned, they are absent. As it is said: "Where there is a single lord, there are five points of friendship. Four points for a Neutral and a Friend. Three points if both are Neutral. One point for a Friend and an Enemy. For a Neutral and an Enemy, the merit is half <note>original: 0.5 points</note>. If the planets are mutual enemies, one should declare zero."

**Now, the analysis of Temperament <term>Gana-vichara</term> is stated.**

> When the man is Godly and the woman is Human, the points are six; 
> the reverse <note>Human man, Godly woman</note> yields five; 
> if the Temperaments are the same, it is six; 
> for a Demon man and a Godly woman, it is one; 
> otherwise, it is zero.

The analysis is as follows: Where the man <note>original: *nara*</note> belongs to the Godly category <term>Deva-gana</term> and the woman <note>original: *vadhu*</note> is of the Human category <term>Manuja-gana</term>, six <note>original: *rasa*</note> points are to be taken. In the reverse case (Human man and Godly woman), five points are taken. If the Temperaments are the same <note>e.g., both are Godly</note>, six points are taken. Where the man is of the Demon category <term>Daitya-gana</term> and the woman is of the Godly category, one <note>original: *bhumi* (earth)</note> point is taken. In all other cases not mentioned, it is zero <note>original: *kha* (space/void)</note>. 

**Now, the analysis of the Merit of the Grid <term>Kuta-guna-vichara</term> is stated.**

The "Twelve"... <note>text cuts off</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical calculation of compatibility points (Gunas) for marriage, specifically addressing what happens when a "bad combination" (Dushkuta) occurs. It transitions from the mechanics of point-scoring to the culturally appropriate ages for marriage for girls and boys, citing various legal and religious authorities.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>
<header>Mu-Ma</header>
<header>Ti-</header>

<margin>Mu-Ma 31</margin> <margin>Ti- 31</margin>

If there is "Friendship of Species" <term>Yoni-maitra</term> and "Distance from the Woman’s Star" <term>Stri-dura</term>, even in a bad combination, then four <note>original: *ambhodhaya*, literally "oceans," a poetic term for the number 4</note> points should be accepted. Otherwise, if there is neither species-friendship nor the proper distance, the merit shall be zero <note>original: *shunya*</note>. If only one of these two—either species-friendship or distance—is present, then one <note>original: *bhu*, literally "earth"</note> point is granted. In the case of an identical sign <term>Rashi</term> or identical star <term>Nakshatra</term>, zero points are taken. Now, even if there is a "Good Combination" <term>Sat-kuta</term>, if there is "Groom-Distance" or mutual enmity between the signs, then six <note>original: *rasa*</note> points are taken. For different signs with the same ruling planet, or different signs with five-point compatibility, five points are taken. In other cases of a "Good Combination," seven <note>original: *giri*, literally "mountains"</note> points are taken.

->**In a bad combination, if there is friendship of species and female-distance, then four points; if not, zero. If only one is present, one point. If signs/stars are identical, zero points. In a good combination, if there is groom-distance or enmity, six points; for different signs with one ruler or five-point match, five points; otherwise in a good combination, seven; and if the "Pulses" differ, eight points. 12. For women, marriage is auspicious for three years starting from the eighth year; for men who have completed their vows, marriage is auspicious at the start of the first or second age-cycle.**<-

Regarding the consideration of the "Pulse" <term>Nadi</term>: if there is a difference in the pulse <term>Nadi-bheda</term>, eight <note>original: *gaja*, literally "elephants"</note> points are taken. If not mentioned, there is an absence of points. This is declared in the *Muhurta Martanda*. **12.**

Now, the time for a woman's marriage is stated: marriage is auspicious during the three years starting from a woman's eighth year. This means it is beneficial in the eighth, ninth, and tenth years. Thus says the Sage Samvarta: "An eight-year-old girl is called Gauri; a nine-year-old is Rohini; a ten-year-old is a Kanya (Maiden); and after twelve, she is considered a Vrishali <note>original: *Vrishali*, a term for a girl who has reached puberty before marriage, often viewed as a ritual lapse</note>." By giving a Gauri in marriage, one attains the celestial world of Vaikuntha; by giving a Rohini, the world of Brahma; by giving a Kanya, the world of Glory; but giving a Vrishali <note>one who has menstruated</note> leads to a different fate. This is also stated in the *Karika*: "A girl should be married within six years; thereafter for two years, the Moon <term>Soma</term> enjoys her; then for the next half-year, the Celestial Musician <term>Gandharva</term> enjoys her, and then the Fire-God <term>Anala</term>." This statement is supported by the Vedic scripture <term>Shruti</term> which begins: "Soma was the first husband..." and ends with "...Soma gave her to the Gandharva, and the Gandharva gave her to Agni." 

However, a contradiction is heard in the *Brahma Purana*, specifically in the "Greatness of Gautami" <term>Gautami-mahatmya</term>, in the dialogue between Vishtibhanu: "Vishtibhanu said: From the fourth year until the tenth, the father must strive to perform the marriage of the girl." This is true, but this Puranic statement refers to the Shudra caste and others, whereas the Vedic and Smriti rules refer to Brahmins; thus, there is no contradiction. The use of even or odd years was not accepted by Sages like Narada for women's marriage; therefore, the Teacher <note>the author of this text</note>, who knows the essence of the scriptures, has stated what is not contradictory for all castes: "For women, from the eighth year, for three years..."

> **One should avoid the month of Jyeshtha for the marriage of the eldest child. One should also avoid the month when the Sun, Mercury, or Jupiter is in the sign of Aquarius <term>Nakhe</term>. In the first pregnancy, one should avoid the month of Jyeshtha for the birth, but not for younger siblings. 13.**

In other texts, it is said that if marriage is not possible during the stated time, it can occur in even years—the twelfth, fourteenth, or sixteenth—due to reasons of gain or lack thereof. In the *Kalanirnaya Dipika*, two times for "Self-Choice" <term>Swayamvara</term> are mentioned: after three years of the onset of puberty <term>Jiva-siddhi</term>, it is the time for self-choice. This applies to all castes if a giver is not available. The "Givers of a Maiden" <term>Kanyadata</term> are listed in the *Kalanirnaya Dipika*: "Father, grandfather, brother, maternal grandfather; if these are not present, three years after the onset of puberty, the girl may choose her own husband." Sage Bodhayana also says: "A girl should wait three years for her father's command after reaching puberty; in the fourth year, she may find a husband equal to herself." 

Now, the time for men's marriage is stated: for men who have completed their sacred vows <term>Cirna-vrata</term>...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of marriage timings and compatibility from page 31, moving from point-scoring (Gunas) to the selection of auspicious years, months, and specific constellations (Nakshatras), as well as the concept of astrological "obstruction" (Vedha).</meta>

<page-num>32</page-num>
<header>Commentary</header>

<margin>Muhurta Martanda 32</margin>
Marriage is auspicious in **odd years** <note>original: *ayugmeṣu viṣameṣu*; refers to the 1st, 3rd, 5th, etc., year of the girl's age or from the onset of puberty</note>. Regarding the specific months mentioned for those years: *Radha* refers to the month of **Vaishakha** <note>April-May</note>, and **Magha** <note>January-February</note> is well-known. Among the pairs of months, the "Radha-pair" consists of Vaishakha and Jyeshtha, and the "Magha-pair" consists of Magha and Phalguna. Marriage is also auspicious in **Margashirsha** <note>November-December</note>. These five months are highly recommended by the authorities. Among these, one should avoid the month of **Jyeshtha** <note>May-June</note> if both the bride and groom are the "firstborn" <term>Jyeshtha-apatya</term> <note>the eldest child of their respective families</note>. However, if only one of them is the eldest, it is not forbidden. He mentions another fault: avoid a time when the Moon <note>original: *himaruci*</note> is in the sign of Gemini <note>or "the month of Jyeshtha" depending on calculation</note> if it is associated with Mercury and Jupiter, or if it is joined with malefic planets or specific unfavorable divisions. This is because of the hearing of undesirable results, as stated in the section on forbidden times. Now, he explains the specific decision regarding the constellations:

> **Mula, the last [Revati], the Sun [Hasta], Magha, the fixed ones [Rohini and the three Uttaras], the Moon [Mrigashirsha], the Wind [Swati], and Maitra [Anuradha] are auspicious for marriage. They should be abandoned by malefic planets, not undergoing the Moon's consumption, and the sky [the star] should not be "pierced" [by Vedha].**
> **[Vedha occurs between] Vishakha and Ashadha; Bharani and Anuradha; the two stars of the directions [Rohini and Abhijit]; Magha and Shravana; mutually between Revati and Uttara Phalguni; Punarvasu and Mula; Shatabhisha and Swati; and both Uttara Bhadrapada and Hasta. 14**

For the sake of clarity, **Narada** says: "Neither in the birth month, nor on the birth star, nor on the birth day should the marriage of the firstborn son or daughter be performed; it destroys the lineage." 13. Now he lists the constellations for marriage: *Mula* is well-known; *Antya* is **Revati**; *Arka* is **Hasta**; *Magha* is well-known; the "fixed ones" are **Rohini** and the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>; *Indu* is **Mrigashirsha**; *Anila* is **Swati**. Along with these, *Maitra* which is **Anuradha**, is auspicious for marriage. Regarding these, he gives a rule: "abandoned by malefic planets" means not joined by "bad" planets. "Not undergoing the Moon's consumption" means it should not be—

---

<margin>32</margin>
—it will not be auspicious. If joined by malefic planets, the "fortune" is "pierced" <term>Vedha</term> <note>an astrological term where one planet's position cancels the benefits of a star</note>, then the sky [the star] is not auspicious. Now he explains the specific "piercing" <term>Vedha</term>: Between **Vishakha** and **Uttarashadha**; between **Bharani** and **Anuradha**; between the "two stars of the directions," which are **Rohini** and **Abhijit**; between **Magha** and **Shravana**—these are mutual. Between **Revati** and **Uttara Phalguni**; between **Punarvasu** and **Mula**; between **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *varuna-bha*</note> and **Swati**; and between **Uttara Bhadrapada** and **Hasta**. These pairs cause mutual obstruction to one another. This connects with the next verse. 14. Now he decides on the "piercing" for specific rituals:

> **This "piercing" [Vedha] is declared for the "grasping of the hand" [marriage] and is not mentioned for others; in those other auspicious acts, it is not spoken of. This Vedha is observed in the Seven-Lath Grid [Saptashalaka Chakra] starting from the direction of Shiva [Northeast] with the star Krittika.**
> **The piercing occurs between the first and last [quarters], the second and third; between Shravana and others... if pierced by a benefic planet, one should avoid that foot [quarter], but if by a malefic planet, avoid it entirely. 17**

It is remembered in the **Seven-Lath Grid** <term>Saptashalaka Chakra</term> <note>a 7x7 line diagram used to determine if planets are in "opposition" or "piercing" specific stars</note>. What kind of grid? One starting from the direction of Shiva <note>Northeast</note>. It begins with the star **Krittika**. This means the calculation of stars follows the zodiac starting from the Northeast corner of the grid. In all marriage-related actions, one must look for this Vedha in the Seven-Lath Grid. Now he explains the "piercing of the quarters" <term>Pada-vedha</term>: the first and fourth quarters, and the second and third quarters obstruct each other. Specifically between Shravana and others, there is mutual piercing. The rule for this is: if a benefic planet <note>Jupiter, Venus, Mercury, or a strong Moon</note> is in the star causing the piercing—

<vocab>Muhurta Martanda, marriage, Nakshatra, Vedha, Saptashalaka Chakra, Astrology</vocab>

<summary>This page details the auspicious timing for marriage, listing favorable years, months, and constellations while explaining the "Vedha" (astrological obstruction) system. It specifically introduces the Saptashalaka Chakra, a grid used to identify conflicting planetary positions that could ruin a marriage's timing.</summary>
<keywords>marriage astrology, Nakshatras, Vedha, Saptashalaka Chakra, Jyeshtha month, firstborn rules, Muhurta Martanda</keywords>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on astrological timing for marriage, specifically focusing on the concept of Vedha (astrological obstruction) and introducing the concept of Latta (planetary kicks), while providing remedies and regional exceptions for these unfavorable conditions.</meta>

<page-num>33</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Martanda 33, Commentary 33</header>

One should avoid the obstruction <term>Vedha</term> caused by the sounds of the wicked <note>original: *khala-rava*; referring here to malefic planets</note>. This means one should avoid all constellations that are "pierced" by such influences. In this context, if an auspicious moment <term>Muhurta</term> is available elsewhere, one should specifically reject the quarter <term>Pada</term> of the constellation that is pierced. As stated by Mandavya: "A constellation pierced by benefic planets should be abandoned for only one quarter, but if pierced by malefic planets, the entire constellation must be rejected." This is the definitive rule of the ancients. According to Vaidyanatha: In the calculation of the two—the seeker and the target—the third quarter should be abandoned if pierced even by malefic planets. Some sages, however, say this applies specifically to difficult sacrificial rites.

Now, he describes the cancellation of the obstruction <term>Vedha-bhanga</term>: When the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagnesha</term> is in the eleventh house <note>original: *bhava-gana-ekadasha*; the house of gains</note>, the fault of the obstruction does not exist. This is the first remedy. Furthermore:

### **If the Lord of the Ascendant is in the eleventh house, or if the Moon is well-aspected, or if benefics are in the Ascendant,**
### **or if it is the planetary hour of a benefic, the ancients say there is no fear of obstruction. Mars and the others strike the 3rd, 20th, 6th, 24th, 8th,**
### **and 20th constellations ahead with a "kick"; the Sun strikes the 12th and the Moon the 7th. This does not apply in the land of Malwa. 16**
### **Avoid the constellation of an eclipse for six months. Avoid the birth constellation and the birth sign if occupied by the Moon or Ascendant.**
### **Avoid the "teeth-like" time units during the transit of the Sun and other planets, as they are considered poisonous**
### **and corrupted. 17**

The second remedy is when the Moon is well-aspected. The third remedy is when benefic planets (excluding the Moon) are positioned in the Ascendant <term>Anga</term>. The fourth remedy is when the timing falls within the planetary hour <term>Hora</term> of a benefic. If any of these conditions are met, the ancient sages say there is no obstruction. Thus Vashistha states: "If the Lord of the Ascendant is joined with or aspected by benefics, or if the Lord is in the eleventh house, or if it is the hour of a benefic, the fault is broken." This is also quoted in the *Antya* text: "Even when there is fear of obstruction, one should consider it nullified if benefics are in the Ascendant or if the Moon is well-aspected."

---

Now he describes the "kick" <term>Latta</term>: Starting from Mars, the planets strike a certain constellation <term>Nakshatra</term> ahead of them with a "kick." Mars strikes the 3rd constellation ahead; Mercury <note>original: *akriti*; 20</note> strikes the 20th; Jupiter strikes the 6th; Venus strikes the 24th <note>original: *jina*; 24</note>; Saturn strikes the 8th; and Rahu <note>original: *nakhabha*; 20</note> strikes the 20th. The Sun <note>original: *arka*</note> strikes the 12th constellation ahead. The full Moon strikes the 7th constellation ahead. However, this breaking of the constellation is not considered in the Malwa region <note>Modern-day Madhya Pradesh, India</note>. It is said: "One should avoid Latta in the Malwa country, the *Pata* fault in Kaushala, and the *Ekargala* fault in Kashmir; but the obstruction <term>Vedha</term> should be avoided everywhere." 16

Now he mentions other faults: Avoid for six months the constellation in which an eclipse <term>Grahana</term> occurred, especially if the Moon or the Ascendant is passing through it. "Moon-passed" refers to the day the Moon is joined with that specific constellation. For example, if an eclipse occurred in Ashwini, and the Ascendant is Aries up to 13 degrees, then Ashwini is in the Ascendant. Similarly for Bharani and other constellations; these should be avoided as "corrupted" constellations. One should also avoid the "pierced" constellation <term>Viddha-rksha</term> and the birth constellation <term>Janma Nakshatra</term>. These should be rejected when they coincide with the birth Ascendant or birth Sign. Furthermore, one should avoid the 14th constellation from the Sun's position.

Next, he states the forbidden time units <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note> during the transit <term>Sankramana</term> of the Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn. These are called "teeth-like units" <term>Rada-damshtra-nadya</term>. Specifically: during the Sun's transit, 32 units <note>original: *rada*; 32</note> are forbidden. This means 16 units before the exact moment of transit and 16 units after. Similarly, for the Moon's transit, certain units are avoided; for Mars, nine units; for Mercury, eight units...

--- Page 37 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining auspicious timing (Muhurta), moving from the concept of Vedha (obstruction) to the specific prohibitions regarding planetary transits, seasonal junctions, and the impurity periods following eclipses and natural portents.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Martanda 34, Commentary 34</header>

<margin>Muhurta Martanda 34</margin>
In the movement <term>Kramana</term><note>Referring to the transit of planets into a new zodiac sign, known as Sankranti.</note>, eighty-four <term>Ghatikas</term><note>A Ghatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note> are considered for Venus, and six Ghatikas for Saturn. For the Moon's transit, one hundred and fifty-seven <note>original: "157"</note> Ghatikas should be avoided; this is the opinion of the experts in Jyotish. In this context, because of the abundance of solar transits, many authorities have prescribed various durations for the transit-hours of the Sun.

Now, he describes the fault of the seasonal junction <term>Sandhi-dosha</term>. Regarding the junction of seasons <term>Ritu-sandhi</term>: sixty-six <note>original: "66"</note> Ghatikas—meaning sixty plus six—at the midpoint of the seasons like Spring <term>Vasanta</term> should be abandoned. According to the *Ratnamala*, which defines the characteristics of the seasons: "From the Sun's transit through two signs starting with Capricorn <term>Mriga</term>, the six seasons occur: Dewy <term>Shishira</term>, Spring <term>Vasanta</term>, Summer <term>Grishma</term>, Monsoon <term>Varsha</term>, Autumn <term>Sharad</term>, and Early Winter <term>Hemanta</term>." Within these, sixty-six Ghatikas are considered the junction. Regarding the junction of lunar days and constellations <term>Tithi-mriku-sandhi</term>, one should separately avoid the junction-hours of lunar days <term>Tithi</term>, lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra</term>, and astrological turnings <term>Yoga</term>. Now, he discusses the previously mentioned impurity caused by eclipses and births.

<insert>
**Sixty-six Ghatikas are to be avoided at the junction of seasons. At the junction of lunar days and stars, and based on the quarters of an eclipse, one should abandon days—three, four, six, or eight—while half of that duration should be abandoned prior to the event.**
</insert>

<margin>Commentary 34</margin>
He describes the characteristics of the eclipse-impurity <term>Grahana-sutaka</term>. From the occurrence of an eclipse <term>Graha</term>, based on the quarters <term>Pada</term> or sections of the eclipse, one should abandon three, four, six, and eight <note>original: "3, 4, 6, 8"</note> days. This is what is meant: if only one quarter of the celestial body is eclipsed, one should abandon three days; if half is eclipsed, four days; if three-quarters are eclipsed, six days; and for a total eclipse, one should abandon eight days. Before the eclipse <term>Prag-grahanat</term>, first, one should abandon half of those days (three, four, etc.). This means: for a quarter-eclipse, one and a half days (ninety Ghatikas) prior to the eclipse should be abandoned; for a half-eclipse, two days; for a three-quarter eclipse, three days; and for a total eclipse, four days prior should be abandoned.

---

One should abandon these before the eclipse. In the *Amarakosha*, it is said regarding the two eclipses: "When the Sun or Moon is seized by Rahu, it is an eclipse." In the *Shardiya Vivaha-patala*: "In a total eclipse, one should avoid all auspicious work for the remaining days. For a three-part eclipse, a few days; for a half-eclipse, four days; and for a quarter-eclipse, three nights is the rule for the Moon and Sun." In this interval, the descending node <term>Pata</term> must also be known. As stated by the Sage Rishiputra: "Three days before an eclipse and seven days after it" refers to the subject of total eclipses. In the *Jyotish-prakasha* as well: "In a total eclipse <term>Khagrasa</term>, for all quarters... eight, six, four, and three days should be known respectively, with half that time prior."

Now, he describes the time to be avoided due to portents <term>Utpata</term>. Regarding portents <term>Utpate-hyaham</term>: When portents such as earthquakes <term>Bhukampa</term>, falling meteors <term>Ulka-nipata</term>, and similar calamities occur, a period of seven days <note>original: "adri-mitanam" - seven, as measured by the seven principal mountains</note> should be abandoned. This means when a portent occurs, one should avoid auspicious deeds for a week. He then describes another fault: the appearance of comets <term>Shikhin</term>.

<insert>
**In the event of a portent, seven days; during the appearance of a comet; the first half of the Full Moon and the Eighth; and the end of the 4th and 11th in the bright fortnight; while in the dark fortnight, these are reduced by one.**
</insert>

One should avoid the appearance of a comet <term>Shikhin</term> or <term>Ketu</term>. As long as the comet is visible, one should not perform any auspicious ceremonies like marriage. So says Vashistha: "Beyond that, one should avoid seven days for a great portent, but avoid the entire duration of a comet's appearance."

Now he describes the period of the "Witch" <term>Bhadra-kala</term> <note>Also known as Vishti, a period considered highly inauspicious for starting new ventures.</note>. The "Witch" occurs in the first half of the Full Moon <term>Raka</term> and the eighth lunar day <term>Ashtami</term>. On the fourth <term>Krita</term> and eleventh <term>Rudra</term> lunar days, the "Witch" occurs in the latter half. These three rules apply to the bright fortnight <term>Abahula</term>. In the dark fortnight <term>Krishna</term>, the "Witch" occurs in the "reduced by one" <term>Nirekeshu</term> positions <note>referring to a specific calculation for the dark half of the month</note>. The exceptions for Bhadra are seen in the *Shardiya Vivaha-patala*, which is why they are not repeated here.

Now he describes the characteristics of the "Joint-End" <term>Gandanta</term><note>The transitional points between water and fire signs/nakshatras, considered spiritually volatile.</note>. These are at the end of the constellations Ashlesha <term>Sarpa</term>, Jyeshtha <term>Indra</term>, and Revati <term>Nigama</term>. From that point in time, one should avoid the Ghatikas immediately before and after the junction.

<summary>This page details the specific durations of inauspicious time to be avoided for ceremonies, including planetary transits, seasonal transitions, and the purification periods required after eclipses and natural portents like earthquakes or comets.</summary>
<keywords>Sankranti, Ritu-sandhi, Grahana-sutaka, eclipse impurity, Utpata, portents, Bhadra, Gandanta, electional astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of inauspicious time-junctions (Sandhi) and introduces specific astrological "faults" or malefic periods known as Gandanta, Ekargala, and the malefic portions of the day like Yamaghanta.</meta>

<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Martanda 35</header>

<margin>Muhurta Martanda 35</margin> <margin>Commentary:</margin> "Always two <term>Ghatikas</term><note>A Ghatika is 24 minutes.</note>" means four Ghatikas in total. This is what is meant: for the stated <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Star constellations or lunar mansions.</note>, two Ghatikas before the end and two Ghatikas after the start—thus four Ghatikas—constitute the Constellation-Junction <term>Nakshatra-Gandanta</term>. Now he describes the Lunar Day Junction <term>Tithi-Gandanta</term>: At the completion of a "Full" <term>Purna</term> lunar day—the 5th, 10th, and 15th—there is a junction of one-and-a-half <note>original: "dvyardhakam," meaning 1.5</note> or two Ghatikas. This junction exists before and after the transition. Now he describes the Ascendant Junction <term>Lagna-Gandanta</term>: For the "creature signs" <term>Keeta</term>—Cancer <term>Karkataka</term>, Pisces <term>Meena</term>, and Scorpio <term>Vrischika</term>—at their conclusion, there is a "short" <term>Ku-ghatika</term> junction. This means half a Ghatika before and half a Ghatika after, totaling one Ghatika. Now he describes the Moon’s Weapon <term>Candayudha</term>: "Sa-vai" refers to the <term>Yogas</term><note>Astrological turnings or alignments.</note> known as <term>Sadhya</term> and <term>Vaidhriti</term>; "Shu" is <term>Shula</term>; "Vya" is <term>Vyatipata</term>; "Ha" is <term>Harshana</term>; "Ga" is <term>Ganda</term>. At the end of these Yogas, the Moon’s mansion becomes the "Moon’s Weapon."

->The junction for the mansions of the Serpent and Indra <term>Ashlesha and Jyeshtha</term> and the final mansion <term>Revati</term> is two Ghatikas; for the "Full" lunar days, it is two or one-and-a-half; for the end of the "creature signs" <term>Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces</term>, it is one Ghatika. The wise avoid these junctions. When the Yogas Sadhya, Vaidhriti, Shula, Vyatipata, Harshana, or Ganda conclude, the mansion of that time becomes the "Moon's Weapon." When specific Yogas align with odd-numbered mansions (counting with Abhijit) from the Sun, it is the "Single Bolt" <term>Ekargala</term>. 19<- <margin>35</margin>

This "Weapon" is like a fierce, strike-heavy tool of destruction; therefore, that Nakshatra must be avoided. Now he describes the "Single Bolt" <term>Ekargala</term>: "In specific Yogas from the day's mansion..." The nine specific Yogas are <term>Vishkambha, Atiganda, Shula, Ganda, Vyaghata, Vajra, Vyatipata, Parigha, and Vaidhriti</term>. When any of these occur, if the Sun is in an odd-numbered mansion <term>Ayugma-bha</term> relative to the day's Nakshatra (counting the intercalary mansion <term>Abhijit</term> as well), then it is Ekargala. <term>Ekargala</term> means "that which bars auspicious results," acting like a single bolt <term>argala</term> blocking a door. 19 Now he describes the fault of the days of the week <term>Vara-dosha</term>: Starting from the Sun, on the days of the week, the portions of the day <term>Yamadala</term> are known by the numbers "Vedas" and so on.

---

The portions of the lunar day <term>Tithyansha</term> are added by the sages. For the Sun (Sunday), it is measured by "Scripture" <note>original: "Nigama," meaning 4</note>; for the Moon (Monday), it is the same <note>4</note>; for Mars (Tuesday), it is "The Twin Physicians" <note>original: "Ashvin," meaning 2</note>; for Mercury (Wednesday), it is "Arrows" <note>original: "Ishu," meaning 5</note>; for Jupiter (Thursday), it is "Serpents" <note>original: "Naga," meaning 8</note>; for Venus (Friday), it is "Three" <note>3</note>; and for Saturn (Saturday), it is "Six" <note>6</note>. These portions are only malefic during the day, not at night. Now he describes <term>Kulika</term> and the others: Starting from the Sun (Sunday), during the day, one should take the 16th part <term>Shodashamsha</term> of the day's length. Among these parts, certain ones are chosen. This is known as <term>Kulika</term>. Others call the 15th part <term>Tithyansha</term> the "Kulika." At night, these are reduced by one <term>Vyekam</term> from the daytime calculation. For example, on Sunday during the day, it is the 14th part, but at night, it is the 13th part. On Monday during the day, it is the 12th, and at night, the 11th, and so on. Now, through these portions...

->From the Sun (on the days of the week), the malefic portions of the day are 4, 4, 2, 5, 8, 3, and 6. The 16th part of the day, or alternatively the 15th part, defines the malefic times like <term>Kulika</term>, <term>Indra</term>, <term>Ravi</term>, <term>Digh</term>, <term>Naga</term>, <term>Ritu</term>, and <term>Veda</term>. These are reduced by one at night. Through these parts, the "Thorn of Time" <term>Kalakantaka</term>, the "Death Bell" <term>Yamaghanta</term>, and <term>Kulika</term> are known relative to Jupiter, Mercury, and Venus. 20<-

This is what is meant: From the planet Jupiter <term>Amarejya</term>, the "Time" <term>Kala</term> is known; from Mercury <term>Gya</term>, the "Thorn" <term>Kantaka</term> is known; and from Venus <term>Asphujit</term>, the "Death Bell" <term>Yamaghanta</term> is known. 20 Now he describes the "Ill-omened Hours" <term>Durmuhurta</term>: Indra <term>Shakra</term> is well-known; the Moon <term>Indu</term>; Jupiter <term>Ijya</term>; and Saturn <term>Yama</term>. On their respective days, the hours corresponding to these names are corrupted or malefic during the day. For Mars <term>Kuja</term>, the 7th hour <term>Anga</term> at night is the ill-omened hour...

<summary>This page details the calculation of ominous "junction" points (Gandanta) between stars, lunar days, and zodiac signs. it also defines complex malefic periods like Ekargala (The Bolt) and various "shadow" moments like Yamaghanta (Death's Bell) which must be avoided for auspicious activities.</summary>

<keywords>Gandanta, Tithi, Nakshatra, Lagna, Candayudha, Ekargala, Kulika, Kalakantaka, Yamaghanta, Durmuhurta, astrological junctions, malefic timing</keywords>

--- Page 39 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for identifying inauspicious astrological timings. While the previous page focused on "junctions" (Gandanta) between signs and lunar days, this page details "Inauspicious Moments" (Durmuhurta), the "Combination of Death" (Mrityu Yoga), and the specific "Toxic Portions" (Visha Ghatika) of each lunar mansion.</meta>

<page-num>36</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Martanda 36</header>

In other texts, many <term>Durmuhurtas</term><note>Literally "bad moments," specific times of the day considered unlucky for starting new ventures.</note> are mentioned, such as those named *Ardhayama*, *Arka*, *Indu*, *Kirana*, *Rakshasa*, and *Brahma*. Here, only a few are listed because I have provided a remedy for them. The <term>Durmuhurtas</term> found in other texts are all contained within the <term>Ardhayama</term><note>A period of approximately 1.5 hours.</note> and other divisions mentioned here. Therefore, I have not listed them separately to avoid making the text unnecessarily long. A wise person will understand this. For example, on Sunday, the fourteenth <term>Muhurta</term><note>A Muhurta is a unit of time lasting 48 minutes; there are 30 in a day.</note> is a <term>Durmuhurta</term>, and the period known as *Kulika* also falls in the fourteenth Muhurta; since they overlap, they are considered together. Other such instances should be analyzed similarly. If one asks, "Were the ancient sages then useless in listing them all?" the answer is no; they were listed to emphasize the varying degrees of severity of the astrological faults. Now, he describes the time when the <term>Pata</term><note>An astronomical astronomical junction involving the sun and moon's declination, considered very inauspicious.</note> occurs, saying "Observe now..." specifically in the fourth quarter of the *Vriddhi* <term>Yoga</term><note>An astronomical alignment of the sun and moon.</note> and the second quarter of the *Brahma* Yoga. Regarding the <term>Mrityu Yoga</term><note>The "Death Combination," a highly malefic alignment.</note>: the Sun and other planets, when paired with Anuradha and other stars, grant death.

->**During the day, the Muhurtas corresponding to the numbers of Indra, the Moon, Jupiter, and Yama are considered corrupted. Others, such as the Ardhayama, are also mentioned in other texts. Observe the Pata now in the fourth quarter of Vriddhi and the second of Brahma. The Sun and other planets, combined with Anuradha, Uttarashadha, Shatataraka, Ashvini, Mrigashirsha, Ashlesha, and Hasta, produce the Yoga of Death. 21**<-

<margin>The Pata (declination junction) should be observed in the specified quarter as taught in the *Brahma Siddhanta* <note>An ancient, authoritative treatise on Indian astronomy.</note>.</margin> 

Now, because it has been said that one should avoid the <term>Mrityu-Yoga</term> ("Death Combination") in all matters, he explains it: the Sun and the following planets, in order, become "givers of death" when paired with these <term>Nakshatras</term><note>Lunar mansions or star constellations.</note>: Anuradha, Uttarashadha, Shatataraka (Shatabhisha), Ashvini, Mrigashirsha, Ashlesha, and Hasta. This means: 
*   **Sun** with Anuradha
*   **Moon** with Uttarashadha
*   **Mars** with Shatataraka
*   **Mercury** with Ashvini
*   **Jupiter** with Mrigashirsha
*   **Venus** with Ashlesha
*   **Saturn** with Hasta 
These combinations result in <term>Mrityu-Yoga</term>. 21

---

Now he describes the <term>Visha-Ghatika</term><note>The "Toxic Moments," a four-ghatika (96-minute) period within each star's duration that must be avoided for auspicious acts.</note>. Starting from the star Ashvini, the toxic period begins after a certain number of <term>Ghatikas</term><note>A Ghatika is 24 minutes.</note>. Specifically, the "toxic" effect lasts for four Ghatikas after the stated starting point. The explanation of these starting points is given in the following list of constants. 22 Now he provides the specific constants for clarifying these times for each star:

| Nakshatra | Starting Ghatika (Constant) | Nakshatra | Starting Ghatika (Constant) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Ashvini** | 50 | **Svati** | 14 |
| **Bharani** | 24 | **Vishakha** | 14 |
| **Krittika** | 30 | **Anuradha** | 10 |
| **Rohini** | 50 | **Jyeshtha** | 15 |
| **Mrigashirsha** | 15 | **Mula** | 56 |
| **Ardra** | 21 | **Purvashadha** | 24 |
| **Punarvasu** | 30 | **Uttarashadha** | 20 |
| **Pushya** | 20 | **Shravana** | 10 |
| **Ashlesha** | 32 | **Dhanishta** | 10 |

After these points, for 4 <term>Ghatikas</term>, there is "poison" (Visha). 23 To calculate the exact time for a specific day, multiply the constant for the star by the actual duration of the star on that day, and divide by 60.

->**Multiply the constant of the star by the current duration of the star and divide by sixty to find the precise starting point. After that precise constant...**<-

...the four "toxic" Ghatikas occur. These should be calculated precisely as described. This means the four Ghatikas of the toxic period must also be adjusted by multiplying them by the star's actual duration and dividing by sixty.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "Toxic Portions" (Visha Ghatika) from the previous page. <page-num>37</page-num>
<header>Muhurta Martanda Commentary</header>

The calculations for these times must be made precise. After determining the exact timing, the four <term>Nadis</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes; four Nadis equal 1 hour and 36 minutes.</note> known as the "Toxic Portions" should be identified. Now, the author describes the exceptions to the "Toxic Portions" (when their bad effects are cancelled): The Moon, if it is in a benefic sign <note>original: "saumya-bhaga"</note>, destroys the fear of poison. Alternatively, if it is aspected <note>A "drishti" or astrological "glance" where one planet influences another's position.</note> by a friendly benefic planet, it destroys the fear of poison. Or, if it is located in its own <term>Navamsha</term><note>The "nine-fold division" of a zodiac sign, used to determine a planet's true strength.</note>, it destroys the fear of poison. Furthermore, if the Moon is located in the "corners" (the 5th or 9th houses), the 7th house, the 10th house, or the 4th house, it likewise destroys the fear of poison.

# The Moon in a benefic sign, or aspected by a friendly benefic, or in its own nine-fold division, or in the 5th, 9th, 7th, 10th, or 4th houses, destroys the fear of poison in this world; so too if the Lord of the Ascendant is thus placed. 23

<gloss>The meaning is as follows:</gloss>
In these positions (5th, 9th, etc.), if the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the event.</note> or the Lord of the Ascendant is present, the danger of the "Toxic Portions" is destroyed. Thus, there are five ways to cancel this fault. Now, one might ask: "A planet in the 7th house is considered forbidden in marriage rituals; how then can the Moon be accepted there?" This is true, but this specific instruction applies to all general activities <note>original: "sarva-karma-sadharanam"</note>. Therefore, for marriage, one should avoid the forbidden positions and accept the others. As the sage Brihaspati says: "The Moon destroys the fault of the Toxic Portions when placed in the centers (1, 4, 7, 10) or trines (1, 5, 9)." Similarly, the text *Phala Pradipa* states: "The Moon in a benefic sign, aspected by a friend, or in its own division—or even the Lord of the Ascendant so placed—destroys the fault arising from the Toxic Portions." 23

---

Now, he describes the unfavorable and favorable houses for planets in a marriage chart: "Moon in the Ascendant," and so on. The Moon or malefics <note>original: "khala," referring to planets like Saturn or Mars.</note> present in the Ascendant lead to sorrow. Likewise, the Moon and Venus in the 6th house; or all planets in the 7th house; or benefics in the 7th house; or Mercury in the 10th house; or the Moon in the 10th house; or Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, a "shadow planet" in Vedic astrology.</note> in the 8th; or the Sun and Saturn in the 4th. Regarding "the one without rays" <note>An epithet for Rahu</note>, the text *Brihat Jataka* provides various names for Rahu. Also, Mars and benefics in the 8th house; and Venus in the 3rd. If these planets are placed in these specific houses, they lead to sorrow.

# The Moon or malefics in the 1st; Moon and Venus in the 6th; all in the 7th; Mercury in the 10th; Rahu in the 8th; Mars and benefics in the 8th; and Venus in the 3rd cause sorrow.
# Conversely, all planets in the 11th; benefics in all houses; and malefics in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th; or the Moon, Sun, and Mercury in the 3rd, 4th, or 2nd, bring prosperity. If these are aspected by benefics, even those in the 8th house do not cause death. 24

<gloss>Now he describes the favorable positions: in the 11th house (the house of gains),</gloss>
all planets are good. Likewise, benefics are favorable in all houses (excepting the forbidden ones). Malefics are good in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses, though Mars in the 8th is still forbidden. The Moon, Sun, and Mercury in the 3rd, 4th, and 2nd houses—each of these brings "Shri" (prosperity and Lakshmi). Thus the favorable and unfavorable planetary positions are explained. He adds a special note: if the Lord of the <term>Navamsha</term>, the Lord of the Ascendant, or the Lord of the <term>Drekkana</term><note>A ten-degree division of a sign, also called a decanate.</note> is located in the 8th house, it may signify death.

...this means they become causes of death <note>original: "mrityu-hetavo-bhavanti"</note>. 24 Now, the author describes the exceptions to certain faults. Sagittarius <note>original: "Taukshika"</note> is the "bow-bearer." A "friend's house" refers to Scorpio <note>original: "Vrischika"</note>, as the Sun and Moon are enemies of Venus. The Sun's house is Leo; the Moon's house is Cancer. If Venus is placed in any of these—Virgo, Sagittarius, or a friend’s house—it does not cause a "break" or failure of the timing, even if it is in the 6th house <note>The 6th house is traditionally associated with obstacles and illness.</note>. It means it does not destroy the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>. As Sage Kashyapa says: "Whether in its sign of debilitation, or in the house of an enemy, or in an enemy’s lunar mansion, Venus in the 6th does not possess the fault of destroying the ritual; of this, there is no doubt." Next, the author describes the exception for the fault of Mars <note>original: "Kuja/Bhauma"</note> in the 8th house: Even if Mars is in the 8th house <note>The 8th house is the "house of death" and usually very unlucky for Mars.</note>, if it is located in the house of Mercury <note>original: "Shashi"</note> or the Moon, or if it is "combust" <note>original: "arkagah"; hidden by the Sun's light</note>, then it does not cause a

### Venus in Virgo, Sagittarius, or a friend’s house does not cause a break even in the 6th; Mars in the house of death (8th) does not cause a break if in the house of Mercury or the Moon, or if combust.

If Mars is in its sign of debilitation <note>Cancer</note>, or in any of these, or is combust, or in the 8th house, it does not destroy the Ascendant. It is said: "With Mars being combust, or even in an enemy's house, the fault arising from Mars in the 8th house does not remain in the slightest." Next, the author describes the exception for the Moon in the 12th or 6th houses: During the bright fortnight <note>The two weeks when the moon is waxing.</note>, the Moon in the 12th or 6th house is auspicious if it has reached its "own divisions" <note>The 10 specific sub-divisions of a sign where a planet gains strength.</note>. This means the Moon is in a favorable division. Such a Moon gives good results provided that Jupiter <note>original: "Ijye/Guru"</note> or Venus is in the Ascendant. How should Jupiter be? "Possessing its own strength" <note>original: "svaujo-yuji"</note>.

---

<margin>38</margin>
"Own strength" means excellent power; one possessed of that is "mightily strengthened." When Jupiter is very strong, the same applies to Venus. The meaning is this: if a strong Jupiter or Venus is in the Ascendant, and the Moon is in the bright fortnight and joined with its own divisions and aspects, then even if it is in the 12th or 6th house, it gives good results and will not cause a "break." Furthermore, in the *Mirror of Auspicious Times* <note>original: "Muhurta Darpane"</note>: "When Jupiter or Venus are situated in strength, and the waxing Moon is in a favorable division and aspected by benefics, it creates success and does not cause a break." This concludes the exceptions for the 6th and 12th houses. Now, he describes the exception for the fault of a "planet-joined" Moon: "Joined with a planet" <note>original: "sagraha"</note> means the Moon is with another planet, excluding Mercury or Jupiter. As was said before, "The Moon joined with planets other than Mercury or Jupiter..." Such a Moon, if aspected by Jupiter in an angle <term>Kendra</term> or its own division,

### The Moon in the 12th or 6th during the bright fortnight is favorable if in its own division and if Jupiter or Venus is strong; the Moon joined with other planets is favorable if aspected by Jupiter in an angle or its own portion. 25

is auspicious. An "angle" is well-known <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses.</note>. If Jupiter is in its own division or an angle and aspects the Moon, the Moon becomes a giver of good results and does not cause a break. It is said: "If Jupiter, staying in its own division, is strong and located in an angle, and aspects the Moon, it causes that fault to dissolve." 25 Next, he describes the exceptions for general faults: Mercury <note>original: "Induja"</note> located in an angle or a "corner" <note>original: "kona"; the 5th or 9th houses</note> destroys a hundred faults. <meta>This page continues the discussion of exceptions (apavada) and the cancellation of astrological faults (bhanga). While the previous page focused on specific planetary placements like Venus in the 6th house, this page shifts to a more general rule about the inherent power of the Moon, Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter to override thousands of negative influences.</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>
<header>Su. Ma. 39</header>
<header>Commentary</header>

The Moon possesses strength. Even if factors like the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> or the Nodes <note>original: "pata-adikân"</note> are poorly placed, if the Moon is powerful, these issues are neutralized. When the Moon or the Sun are endowed with strength in the Ascendant, regardless of the time of day, the ritual may proceed. Now, the author speaks of the "Cancellation of Faults" <note>original: "dosha-apavada-bhanga"</note>. These cancellations occur when the faults themselves become "powerless" <note>original: "vibala"</note>. In this context of cancellation, those faults no longer produce their ill effects; they are incapable of causing harm. Therefore, one should judge the auspiciousness of a time only after considering these cancellations. As it is said: "Commonly mentioned faults are destroyed by these cancellations if they occur..."

**Mercury <note>original: "Induja," the son of the Moon</note> cancels a hundred faults. Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note> cancels a hundred. Jupiter <note>original: "Shankra"</note>, if positioned in an angle <term>Kendra</term> or a trine <term>Trikona</term>, cancels a hundred thousand <note>original: "laksham"</note> faults. If the Moon is strong and the Ascendant is radiant, the faults of the nodes and others are broken. Those faults become powerless. 26**

**Those faults are ineffective. A planet at the junction <term>Sandhi</term> between houses provides no result. A planet at the degree of the house-center <term>Bhava-madhya</term> provides its full result. A planet positioned beyond the junction gives the result of the next house. 26 The degrees are calculated by the "bent hours" <note>original: "nata-nadika"</note> multiplied and then added or subtracted to the Sun’s position; the result is the 10th house, and the Ascendant plus six signs is the 7th house.**

<margin>39</margin>
These faults become weak; they lose their ability to manifest. Now the author explains the "purity of the houses" <note>original: "bhava-shuddhi"</note>. A planet equal to the degrees of the junction <term>Sandhi</term> is fruitless; it gives neither good nor bad results. A planet equal to the degrees of the house-center <note>original: "bhava-amsha"</note> provides its entire result. The specific results for intermediate positions should be known through mathematical proportion. Regarding the specific placement: a planet that has moved past the junction point belongs to the next house. 26 When it is said "equal to the junction," one might ask: what are these junctions and what are these houses? To address this, the author provides a method for calculation in one verse: "The degrees are..."

---

The degrees are calculated using the "bent" <note>The "bent" or "noon-distance" (nata) refers to the time elapsed before or after local noon.</note> hours. By calculating the desired time from the Sun's position, and then multiplying by six, one finds the degrees. Depending on whether it is before or after noon (east or west), one subtracts or adds this to the Sun's position at that moment. This calculation reveals the Midheaven or 10th House <note>original: "dashama-bhava"</note>. From that point, adding six signs reveals the 4th House. The Ascendant <term>Lagna</term> is calculated as previously described, and adding six signs to it reveals the 7th House <note>original: "mada," the house of the partner</note>. Having established these four primary "pivot" houses, the author explains how to find the internal houses (the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, etc.) by dividing the space between them:

**Take the difference between the Ascendant and the 10th house and divide it by three; add these parts to the Ascendant to find the 2nd and 3rd houses and their junctions. Adding six signs to these provides the remaining houses. 27 If a planet is surrounded by cruel planets <note>original: "kurayoh," referring to malefics like Mars or Saturn</note>, it is in the "Scissors" <term>Kartari</term> position. This applies regardless of whether the motion is direct or retrograde; however, the influence of Jupiter in the 12th house is auspicious.**

<margin>39</margin>
The 7th house is established. By taking the degrees of the 10th house minus the Ascendant and dividing by three, one gets the "three parts" <note>original: "tri-lava"</note>. This is done to find the houses in between. One part is added to the Ascendant to create the 2nd house. Two parts added to the Ascendant create the 3rd house. Similarly, by using the 4th house as a base, the 5th and 6th houses are calculated. These six houses, when increased by six signs (180 degrees), reveal the opposite six houses.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating the strength and positions of the astrological houses, specifically focusing on the junctions between houses and the "Scissor" fault (Kartari), as well as the calculation of the "Fivefold Fault" (Panchaka). It follows the previous discussion on how certain planetary strengths can override general faults.</meta>

<page-num>40; 50</page-num>
<meta>image quality: excellent</meta>

<margin>Original Manuscript Page 40</margin>
<margin>Commentary Page 50</margin>

The <term>Bhava</term> <note>the twelve houses of a horoscope</note> are to be calculated by adding six zodiac signs; these are to be known from the seventh house. This means from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or 1st house</note> through the six houses ending at the seventh <note>original: "mada," a term for the 7th house</note>. These are the houses such as the Ascendant, the Fourth <note>original: "sukha," meaning happiness/4th house</note>, and the Tenth <note>original: "jna," meaning knowledge/10th house</note>. Now, the author explains the method for calculating the <term>Sandhi</term> <note>the junction or cusp between two houses</note>. The junction is the sum of two houses divided by two; this is clearly established. Now, regarding the explanation of the alternative reading: when the tenth house is calculated without the seventh, the <term>Lagna</term> degree and the signs of the six houses are noted separately. These are subtracted from one another. These values are then repeatedly added to the First <note>original: "anga," meaning body</note> and Fourth <note>original: "patala," meaning the underworld/nadir</note> houses. With these additions, the six houses including their junctions are determined. These houses, when added to the other six, complete the set. After calculating the house junctions in this way, one must consider the planetary results, as it was previously stated that the result of a planet is proportional to its distance from the junction. Following this, one must observe...

<insert>
## Even if there is a benefic planet in its exaltation, or if Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury are in the angles or trines, if they are the "Scissors" <note>the planets forming the Kartari fault</note> or if they are in their sign of debilitation, they do not provide protection. 28 In the Ascendant, combined with the lunar day <term>Tithi</term>, the degrees are divided by nine. Based on the remainders—8, 2, 4, 6, 1—these represent Disease, Fire, the King, the Thief, and Death. Do not perform rituals regarding illness, the Sun, or the sacred thread ceremony during these times.
</insert>

...it is known that the result of a planet is determined by the interval between the <term>Sandhi</term> <note>house junction</note> and the planet's actual position. 
This is the rough method of house calculation. The precise method should be understood through mathematical tables. 29 Now, the author explains the characteristics and exceptions of the "Scissor" fault <term>Kartari-Dosha</term> in a single section. From the Moon or from the Horizon <note>the Ascendant</note>, if malefic planets occupy the 12th house <note>original: "vyaya"</note> and the 2nd house <note>original: "sva"</note>, it is called the "Scissor" fault <note>where the house or planet is "squeezed" between two harms</note>. Now, the characteristics of the "Great Scissor" <term>Maha-Kartari</term> are explained: if a malefic in the 2nd house is retrograde <term>Vakri</term> and a malefic in the 12th house is moving direct <term>Riju</term>, then it is a Great Scissor fault <note>because both planets are moving toward the house in between</note>. Thus, the characteristics of the Scissor fault are stated.

---

The cancellation of that fault is now explained: If a benefic planet is present in those positions, that Scissor fault does not exist—this is the first cancellation. Or, if a benefic planet—either Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus—is in the Ascendant, the fault is cancelled—this is the second cancellation. If the Moon is in the 2nd house, the fault is cancelled—this is the third cancellation. Or, if a benefic planet is in the 2nd house, the fault is cancelled—this is the fourth cancellation. Or, if Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury are in the angles <term>Kendra</term> <note>houses 1, 4, 7, 10</note> or trines <term>Trikona</term> <note>houses 5, 9</note>, then by the power of time, the Scissor fault will not occur—this is the fifth cancellation. Or, if the two malefic planets causing the Scissor fault are in their signs of debilitation <note>original: "nica"</note>, then the fault does not exist—this is the sixth cancellation. 28

Now, the author explains the "Fivefold Fault" <term>Panchaka</term> and its exceptions. In the Ascendant, one takes the current lunar day <term>Tithi</term> and the number of the Ascendant, adds them, and divides by nine. With the remainders—8, 2, 4, 6, 1—the results are respectively: Disease, Fire, the King, the Thief, and Death. 
"Disease" is well-known. 
"Fire" is well-known. 
"King" <note>original: "Ina"</note> refers to the ruler, the sovereign, the master, the lord <note>The text lists synonyms: Raja, Adhipa, Pati, Swami, Natha, Parivridha, Prabhu, Ishvara, Vibhu, Ishana</note>. 
"Thief" <note>original: "Hara"</note> refers to one who takes away. 
"Death" <note>original: "Atyaya"</note> refers to the end of life. 
These are the five faults <term>Panchaka</term>. This is what is meant: add the Ascendant and the lunar day (starting from the first day of the waxing moon), divide by nine; if the remainder is 8, it is Disease; if 2, it is Fire; if 4, it is the King; if 6, it is the Thief; if 1, it is Death. 

Now, he says where these should be avoided. Regarding "Disease," avoid it at night, on Sundays, or during the sacred thread ceremony <term>Maunji</term>. Regarding "Fire," avoid it on Tuesdays and Thursdays. "King" or "Authority" <term>Ina</term> should be avoided during the day on Saturdays or when serving a master, and on Mondays. Regarding "Thief," avoid it on Thursdays and...
